BASS-II:O18 | PSI-25 | pmma | 6.35-mm clear rod |  | micro |  | rod |  | 6.35 | 14.5 |  | 15.2 |  |  |  | pot 1.0 | stagnation | –31 | went-out |  |  |  | MEASURED | BAS-01 | 118 | Table A.5 | as-run test no.=102; sample no.=212m; stag region blew out at pot 4.0 and restabilised at 1.5; second time it did not; PI sheet=Olson / Two local blowoffs on one 0.318 cm rod (Fig. 6.7 caption 'two local blowoffs for 0.318 cm rod'; p.81). Blowoff 1: flow turned up, flame hole ~9 mm, stagnation region re-established (hole closed 7 s after the flow was turned down). Blowoff 2, 55 s later: 10-mm hole 'persisted for over a minute even after the flow was turned down again to the initial flow before the flame destabilized and blew off completely' (p.81) -> outcome went-out (blowoff); XO2 final = 14.5 percent for this test (p.81). Stretch rate history (Fig. 6.7 'Stretch rate'; tol ±2.5 s, ±0.5 s^-1): baseline ~6 s^-1 before Blowoff 1 and after ~558 s; peak ~33 s^-1 (Blowoff 1, ~457-460 s); plateau ~10 s^-1 after each turn-down; peak ~32 s^-1 (Blowoff 2, ~522 s); 'Flame extinction' at ~600 s with stretch rate=~6 s^-1. No test number printed: may be BASS-II:O18 (BAS-01 Table A.5 p.118: 6.35-mm clear rod, calibrated final vol% O2 14.5; observations: stag region blew out at pot 4.0, restabilized at 1.5, second time would not restabilize); not merged.

BASS-II:O4 | PSI-25 | pmma | 9.525-mm clear rod |  | micro |  | rod |  | 9.53 | 13.6 |  | 14.8 |  |  | 1 |  | stagnation | –32 | went-out |  |  |  | MEASURED | BAS-01 | 119 | Table A.5 | as-run test no.=66; sample no.=216m; how it went out not stated; PI sheet=Olson; fan display (G)=5, 0.7, 0.5, 0.4, 0.36 ext / Lowest O2 quenching test (p.77: 'the lowest O2 quenching test, where the initial O2 setting was 14.8 percent O2 (by volume) and the final O2 reading was 13.6 percent O2 ... this r = 0.476 cm rod radius test'). Flow decreased incrementally; flame steady at each flow until the last one leading to extinction (p.77); quenching extinction over 100 s after the last flow change (p.78); aspect ratio 0.5 at extinction; regression rate 0.0385 mm/s. Flow steps from Fig. 6.5 'Flow' series (tol ±30 s, ±0.25 cm/s): ~4.5 (ignition ~90 s to ~320 s), ~2.1 (to ~585 s), ~1.5 (to ~835 s), ~1.15 (to ~1,100 s), ~1.0 cm/s (last step, to ~1,220 s); text names the '2- to 1.5-cm/s flow change' (p.77). Flame quenched at ~1,219.6 s from ignition start (Fig. 6.6, B6 s36). Start flow = first step; ignition 'typically at a flow speed of 5 cm/s' (p.75). No test number printed: may be BASS-II:O4 (BAS-01 Table A.5 p.119: 9.525-mm clear rod, calibrated initial vol% O2 14.8, final 13.6; observations 'Larger rod, decreased flow, extinguished at 0.36'); not merged.

Candidate PSD-01 as-run row BASS:1 ('as run matrix' row 4: SIBAL 2 cm, fan display 66 > 52, air display 10, 5; actual date 2012-03-30, video display date 03/31/2012 per sheet '2012'); not merged. / p.38 backup plot (no title): Flame tip, Flame Base, Flame Length vs 'Time, s from igniter start', annotations 'Ignition' and 'Flow change from 10 cm/s to 5 cm/s'; sample width and flow direction not stated on the slide. Values (B10 s10 corrected by work/corrections.tsv to 'local minimum'; tol ±5 s, ±10 mm left, ±1 mm right): Ignition line ~1 s; flow change line ~23 s; Flame Length first ~13 mm at ~4 s, maximum ~41 mm at ~22.7 s just before the flow change, ~10 to ~12 mm between ~26.5 and ~31 s, last point ~1 mm at ~42.2 s (curve ends); Flame tip first ~99 mm (base ~112 mm), local minimum ~32 mm at ~22.8 s, local maximum ~48 mm at ~26.5 s, last ~22 mm at ~42.2 s (base ~23 mm). Flame state at 5 cm/s and the end are not stated in words: outcome not-reported. Near the end of the burn, a jet of nitrogen is imposed but it fails to extinguish the flame. (Solid is very warm.) Finally after a series of flow reductions, the flame goes out when the flow speed is zero.' Nitrogen suppression failed (not 'suppressed'); went out when the flow was reduced to zero. Nitrogen-jet flow rate not printed. Shape not named on p.52 (sample_form NR; p.53/p.58 name PMMA spheres but are not linked to p.52). o2 '17%' and diluent 'N2' split from the printed '17% O2/N2'. gravity and platform from p.2. Arrow 'Flow' points up in the image; direction relative to the flame not stated. [S184]; [S96]. Identifiers: PMMA, 15 mm initial diameter, 17% O2/N2, 1 atm, <1 cm/s, nitrogen jet near end, out at zero flow.

BAS-01:nodate:PMMA-ROD-ISS-BOUNDARY-MINIMUM:df589e15 | PSI-25 | pmma | cast PMMA |  | micro |  | rod |  |  | 13.5 | y |  |  |  |  |  | with flame | hot-wire igniter at the initially hemispherical stagnation tip | limit | limit |  |  | MEASURED | BAS-01 | 83 | Eq. 6.1 | minimum of the microgravity flammability boundary (document: 'The minimum in this boundary occurs at a = 8 to 12 s–1 at approximately 13.5 percent O2', p.83; p.84: 'The minimum O2 concentration of 13.5 percent O2 at a of 8 to 12 s–1 is significantly lower than the observed upward flame spread limit of 15.4 percent O2'; p.97). stretch rate=8 to 12 s-1 (a = (3/2)(U/r)(r/ro)^1/2). Boundary = combined empirical fit Eq. 6.1 X_O2 = 6exp(−0.5a) + 0.0167a + 13.4 (p.83); O2 = final O2. BAS-09 Fig. 6 plots the 'BASS-II boundary' [12] (= Olson and Ferkul, 9th U.S. National Combustion Meeting, i.e. BAS-04) with its minimum at ~10 s-1 adjusted stretch rate (tol ±25 s^-1) and gives the minimum O2 as 13.6% in text (p.8, see the 13.6 % row): CITED [12].
BAS-01:nodate:PMMA-ROD-ISS-LOWEST-FINAL-O2-SUSTAINED:23bb3421 | PSI-25 | pmma | cast PMMA |  | micro |  | rod |  |  | 13.6 |  |  |  |  |  |  | with flame | hot-wire igniter at the initially hemispherical stagnation tip | limit | limit |  |  | MEASURED | BAS-01 | 76 |  | lowest final O2 at which flames survived / minimum test point (document BAS-01 p.76: 'flames ignited at a higher O2 survived to final O2 concentrations as low as 13.6 percent O2'; BAS-04 p.7: 'the minimum test point of 13.6 % O2'; BAS-09 p.8: 'the observed 13.6% O2 minimum concentration needed to get stable flames in BASS-II [12]', CITED, [12] = S.L. Olson, P.V. Ferkul, 9th U.S. National Combustion Meeting = BAS-04). Stretch rate and rod size not stated with the value. BAS-01 Fig. 6.9 lowest-O2 'ISS stable flame' point is at ~13.6 vol% (separate figure-point row); BAS-04 Fig. 4 and BAS-01 Fig. 6.9 also show quench/blowoff points at ~13.5 (below this value; reader flag).
BAS-01:nodate:PMMA-ROD-ISS-SUSTAINED-IGNITION-LIMIT:577ceb4b | PSI-25 | pmma | cast PMMA |  | micro |  | rod |  |  | 14.5 | y |  |  |  | 5 |  | with flame | hot-wire igniter at the initially hemispherical stagnation tip | limit | limit |  |  | MEASURED | BAS-01 | 76 | Fig. 6.2 | sustained-ignition limit, initial O2 (document: 'Below an initial O2 concentration of ~14.5 percent O2, a sustained ignition could not be achieved even though flames were observed when the igniter was on and briefly after the igniter was turned off before extinguishing'; 'the ignition limit of ~14.5 percent O2', p.76). Flow: 'Ignition generally occurred at a forced flow of 5 cm/s' (p.76); unsustained attempts also tried initial flows up to 10 cm/s and down to 2 cm/s (p.75). Ignition limit is above the extinction limit: flames ignited at higher O2 survived to 13.6 percent (p.76); p.97 'Ignition limits are found to be at higher O2 concentrations than the flammability limits'. Fig. 6.2 dashed box 'Observed O2 ignition limit' right edge read ~14.7 to ~14.8 vol% initial O2 (tol ±0.25 vol%; reader flags the difference with ~14.5). O2 basis: initial readings calibrated with the onboard mass spectrometer (p.75).

Claim MEASURED by default: the deck attributes the value to no other work. p.27 prints "At 1g ULOI, but narrower sample" for the ISS Nomex-III burn titled "NOMEX – III, 22% O2, 1 atm 14 cm/s (0g)"; p.24 itself states no gravity for the value, so gravity stays blank. / gravity_cat: Earth is the team's reading, not printed with the value: BAS-03 p.24 prints 'ULOI: 22% O2' for Nomex-III with no gravity, igniter or pressure, and p.27 says of the ISS burn at 22 % O2 'At 1g ULOI, but narrower sample' (was ''; team ruling R, 15 Nov 2026) / gravity_basis: see gravity_cat (was ''; team ruling R, 15 Nov 2026)

Air flow speed, sphere diameter, pressure and time between images not printed. o2 'air' as printed. gravity and platform from p.2. [S184]; [S96]. Identifiers: PMMA sphere, wake configuration, air, nitrogen 500 cc/min.

CITED: data from ref. [15] ('The microgravity experiments were conducted in the Orbital ATK Cygnus spacecraft (Saffire II) [15]', p.5; legends 'μg - Saffire II [15]'). spread_rate = Fig. 11 series 'μg - Saffire II [15]' ~0.09 mm/s, single point plotted at 100 kPa, no error bar (verified ~fig; tol ±0.1 mm/s); p.11: 'the flame spread rate data of Saffire II obtained from the flame tip progress shown in Fig. 7'. Fig. 12 and Fig. 13 plot the same point (~0.09 mm/s). Pressure printed 'atmospheric pressure' (p.6); the abstract prints 'Microgravity … Cygnus spacecraft at 100 kPa with an air flow velocity of 200 mm/s' (p.1). Outcome burned: 'The PMMA sample was burned in air with a 200 mm/s flow and atmospheric pressure' (p.6). Flame tip trace (Fig. 7, p.9, verified ~fig): starts ~48 mm, minimum ~11 mm at ~20-50 s, ~75 mm at 450 s. Igniter 'larger than that used in normal gravity to assure ignition of the thick fuel' (p.6).

SOF-03 Table 1 (p.3): case #=2; extinction mode=blowoff; Re=914.1; flow strain rate=25.71 1/s; conductive heat loss=0.95 (W/cm2). The Table 1 values are the local (stagnation-point) blowoff: 'Extinction starts with a flame hole at the stagnation point (Fig. 4(b)). This local flame blowoff occurs when oxygen is below the limit (14.99%) for this particular stagnation flow strain rate (25.7 s-1)' (p.4); then two flame-base pulsation cycles and azimuthal spinning for 3.18 s 'before complete blowoff extinction' (p.4-5); O2 at complete blowoff not printed in text. Burning event: 'at 200 s, flame is still away from extinction at 16.6% O2' (p.4, Case #2 = 34.28 cm/s flow). Possibly the same point as SOF-02 Fig. 8 stagnation blowoff at ~34.3 cm/s, ~15.0 %, qc 0.95 (inferred, not merged). [S248]; [S325]. [S282]; [S329]. [S252]

# SHARED NOTES
ref | text
[S1] | MOC = all samples (at least five) pass the burn length criterion, ULOI by the up-and-down method for small samples, 14.7 psia except as noted (p.3)
[S2] | initial quiescent, closed 1400-L chamber with vacuum, oxygen and nitrogen supplies, ASTM D 2863 steps of 1% oxygen by volume (p.2)
[S3] | 1-g upward flammability threshold test (p.2: 'selecting worst case 1-g flammability test conditions')
[S6] | Gravity, platform, diluent, sample width and thickness not printed with the table (gravity NR; p.2 says 'NASA relies on a 1-g test for screening material flammability for flight'). flow_direction 'upward' from the cited title
[S7] | Table 2 (p.16): 'NASA-STD-6001B Flammability Test 1 Maximum Oxygen Concentration Sample Length Impact', cited from Tapia Harper, Juarez, Woods and Beeson, 'NASA-STD-6001B Test 1 Upward Flame Propagation
[S9] | Sample length header 'Sample Length cm / in.' -> sample_size. basis 'measured' from the footnote 'All O2 Concentration measurements were made to a tolerance of +0.15%/-0.0 %'.
[S10] | Footnote a: 'ULOI (Upward Limiting Oxygen Index) is a variation of ASTM D28632 Limiting Oxygen Index (LOI) utilizing the NASA-STD-6001B test fixture and orientation'
[S11] | O2 cells printed without a unit - o2_unit blank
[S14] | No test number: merge by campaign, sample width, flow direction and speed only. p.22 right plot 'Flame Spread Rate cm/s' vs 'Concurrent Flow cm/s' (left) / 'Opposed Flow cm/s' (right)
[S15] | Test 1 passing criteria (p.3): 'no specimen of the five required test specimens burns more than 6 inches, and no test specimen propagates a flame through the transfer of burning debris'. enclosure and ignition copied from the Test 1 description stated once for the method (p.3, cited [9]): 'a vertically oriented specimen (typical size 2.5 inch x 17 inch) is exposed to a chemical ignitor flame at its bottom for approximately 25-30 seconds
[S16] | 'Extinction boundary shaded in green that divides steady flames from extinguished flames in centrifuge drop tests.' Photo labels: '¼" PMMA rod, ignited in 1g by retractable hot wire'
[S18] | 1g blowoff limit (document: 'The lowest flow rate where blowoff occurs was determined iteratively for each oxygen concentration', p.2; 'the bracketing flow conditions were repeated at least once', p.3)
[S19] | Sample Length Impact on MOC Investigation', 47th ICES (2017) (reference printed under the table)
[S22] | footnote a: 'PET and PEI were tested at 10.2 psia in a GRC fixture and ignited with a hot wire following the GRC protocol described in footnote a of Table 2' (sample size from that footnote, p.5)
[S23] | The Fig. 3 x axis is 'forced flow velocity, cm/s' (the text defines U as the average flow speed entering the tube, p.3; maximum 165 SLPM = 60.3 cm/s average tube speed, p.2)
[S24] | Fig. 8: 'Map of extinction boundary of diffusion flames over a 4-cm PMMA sphere in one-atmosphere in microgravity purely forced flows' (caption, p.8)
[S26] | 'In all tests, the pressure and oxygen percentage in the MSG remain constant at ISS ambient values of 1.0 atm and ~22%' (daily O2 21.3% to 22.9%) (p.4)
[S27] | gravity from the text p.7 ('flammability limits of thermally thin solid materials in a microgravity environment')
[S28] | footnote b: 'MOC (Maximum Oxygen Concentration) per NASA-STD-6001B, Test 1'
[S29] | Procedure: rod ignited in dry shop air outside the apparatus, gas then switched to the premixed O2/N2 test gas at the same flow rate (p.2)
[S30] | LOC determined by the up-and-down method, flammable = flame passes the reference line 60 mm from the holder bottom (p.10–11)
[S31] | outcome: steady flame spread ('the flame reaches a steady length and spread rate at low flow speeds (< 15 cm/s) for all tested inter-baffle distances', abstract p.1; samples 'allowed to burn to completion')

# TESTS NOT IN THIS TABLE
The dashboard's charts also show 173 gas-burner tests of the ACME BRE experiment (PSI-20; test IDs starting "BRE:"; materials: Ethylene-nitrogen gas (burner); Methane-nitrogen gas (burner)). Their rows and the burner documents are not in this context: they are covered by the dashboard's burner memory, which answers when the user selects a burner test, the ACME BRE experiment or a burner gas on the dashboard, or names the burner in the question. If a question needs those tests, the lead says that the burner tests are on the chart and that selecting one of them (or the experiment) gets the answer from the burner documents; never guess their values from this context. In an answer, say "the burner documents"; never write "memory" or "context".
